COUNCIL SPECIAL MEETING MINUTES

August 5, 2026 at 6:30 P.M.

Council Chambers

Council Members: President T. Harriman, T. Arnold, J. Eudaily, A. Lewis, J. Sintz, M. Terry

Mayor: Jody Carney Village Administrator: Haley Lupton

Police Chief: Dale McKee  Director of Finance: Renee Sonnett  Director of Law: Paul-Michael La Fayette

Call to Order: Mr. Harriman called the meeting to order at 6:30 P.M., followed by the Pledge of Allegiance.

Present: Toni Arnold, Jim Eudaily, Tyler Harriman, Aaron Lewis, Jamey Sintz, and Michael Terry.

Absent: Jody Carney.

Approvals:

Agenda: Council Special Meeting – August 5, 2026

The August 5, 2026 agenda was approved by unanimous consent.

Minutes: Council Regular Meeting – July 13, 2026

Mr. Sintz motioned to approve the July 13, 2026 minutes, seconded by Ms. Arnold. All in favor. The July 13,

2026 minutes are approved.

Discussion Items:

Development Intern Presentation:

Ms. Emily Shoup, the Development Intern, thanked Council for their time this evening. She provided an

overview of her primary internship project for the summer, which was an Uptown parking study and

analysis. Ms. Shoup discussed the process used in completing the parking study, which included an

inventory of all Uptown parking spaces, such as on-street and off-street parking, and ADA parking spaces.

Additionally, vacancy rates were analyzed. Ms. Shoup noted that events hosted by Uptown businesses

impacted parking vacancy rates, with an example taking place on a Thursday evening. During such events,

parking availability was very limited. Ms. Shoup stated that she shared the results of the parking study with

800 Village Boulevard | P.O. Box 167 | Plain City, OH 43064 | 614-873-3527 | plaincityohio.gov

the GoUptown! organization and collected their feedback as part of the parking study analysis. Ms. Shoup

discussed the pedestrian experience as it relates to parking availability and provided recommended

solutions to enhance the pedestrian experience. Such potential solutions include installing wayfinding

signage, improving the streetscape to provide improved separation between vehicles and pedestrians,

signal timing improvements, and constructing additional signalized crosswalks. Ms. Shoup provided

recommendations to improve accessibility to parking areas as well. Ms. Arnold inquired if ADA-compliant

parking spaces could be installed in on-street parking spaces. Ms. Shoup stated that it is possible but

given the required amount of clearance surrounding an ADA-compliant parking space, it is often not

feasible due to the physical constraints of a downtown thoroughfare, such as Main Street in the Uptown

area. Ms. Arnold thanked Ms. Shoup for the information and recommended implementing ADA-compliant

parking spaces on North Chillicothe Street or South Chillicothe Street, if space allows.

Ms. Shoup reviewed suggestions to increase the turnover rate of parking spaces, thereby creating more

parking space availability. Her suggestions include implementing maximum time limits for parking spaces

or adding a required payment system for parking areas. Ms. Shoup provided additional information

regarding potential processes for time limits or paid parking areas, as well as a “parking benefit district”

(PBD). Ms. Shoup noted that all recommendations include supporting documentation and resources within

the completed parking study. Additional recommendations include employee parking permits such that

they would not have to pay for parking and implementing neighborhood parking programs in which

residential streets are improved to allow on-street parking. Ms. Shoup recommended creating and

advertising a parking lot map, and publishing such map on the Village of Plain City website. Rideshare

programs should also be encouraged, especially for events hosted by Uptown businesses. Ms. Shoup

provided information regarding payments in-lieu of parking which would require businesses to pay a fee if

the required number of parking spaces for their business cannot be constructed due to physical

constraints. The collected fees are then used by the Village to construct parking infrastructure

improvements in the Uptown area. Ms. Shoup also discussed electric vehicle (EV) chargers in the Uptown

area and noted that, once installed, usage should be monitored accordingly. Ms. Shoup completed her

presentation by summarizing recommended action items and strategies, which include suggested future

parking expansion areas and a possible parking structure. Ms. Shoup thanked Council for allowing her to

complete her internship with the Village and stated that all staff has been exceptional.

Mr. Terry inquired if the parking study includes The Depot at Public Square area as a tentative location for

a parking structure. Ms. Shoup confirmed that the parking study does include that area as a possible

location.

Mr. Terry stated his support for implementing a timed parking system, primarily for on-street parking. The

difficulty of enforcing such time limits was acknowledged. Mr. Terry recommended that on-street parking

be limited to two hours, with the understanding that such limit would decrease overnight on-street

parking. Mr. Terry also stated his support for implementing wayfinding signage as well as a 24-hour limit

for off-street parking lots. Mr. Terry stated that he would not be in favor of paid parking, especially in that

it would place a financial burden on residents. The safety aspect of allowing overnight parking in off-street

lots was discussed, noting that patrons of Uptown restaurants would be less likely to operate a vehicle

while impaired if they know that their vehicle would not be towed while in a permitted parking lot. Chief

McKee stated his support for that approach as well. Mr. Eudaily stated that he is also not in favor of

enacting paid parking but is supportive of the two hour on-street parking time limit. Mr. Harriman noted

that he also would not be in favor of a paid parking system. Mr. Eudaily stated that event employees and

volunteers should be required to park in an area other than Uptown in order to create more parking

availability for visitors and tourists. Mr. Terry stated his support for a payment in-lieu system. Mr. Eudaily

noted that many small businesses would not be able to afford such a system. Ms. Arnold inquired if it is

800 Village Boulevard | P.O. Box 167 | Plain City, OH 43064 | 614-873-3527 | plaincityohio.gov

possible to require an event organizer to pay a fee due to the increased parking area usage during an

event. Ms. Shoup responded that she did not find an example of such a requirement in her research. Mr.

Terry stated that applying such a requirement could be difficult given the complexity in determining which

events should be subject to the requirement. Ms. Arnold stated that the system could require an add-on

parking fee to ticketed events, which would be collected by the event organizer. The collected fees would

then be provided to the Village and utilized for parking area infrastructure improvements. Mr. Derek

Hutchinson, the Village Planner, stated that the payment in-lieu system is currently implemented for all

parts of the Village, with the exception of the Uptown area due to the physical constraints of the area. Mr.

Harriman inquired if a tax or fee system specific to the Uptown area could be implemented as an

alternative to a payment in-lieu system. Mr. Terry acknowledged the importance of maintaining a balance

between the various suggestions. It was noted that the current parking demand is an indication that

tourism in the Uptown area has increased drastically as compared to ten years ago, which is beneficialfor

the Village and local businesses. Mr. Harriman stated his support for installing wayfinding signage and

publishing an online parking lot map. Mr. Terry acknowledged that parking availability will always be an

important topic for discussion but that the Village needs to be creative in its solution-finding. Mr. Terry

reiterated that he prefers to not charge residents and visitors for parking in the Uptown area. Mr. Sintz

noted that designated employee parking areas may be necessary as businesses grow. Ms. Arnold stated

that the aforementioned events on Thursday evenings occur frequently and do pose concerns to parking

availability. Additionally, she stated her support for publishing a parking lot map and to address the parking

concerns as quickly as possible.

All of Council thanked Ms. Shoup for her hard work and service this summer. Mr. Hutchinson stated that

Ms. Shoup has been an excellent intern and that staff would not have had time to complete the parking

study without the hard work of Ms. Shoup.

E-Bikes and Powered Scooters:

Ms. Lupton stated that the proposed code draft regarding e-bicycles and powered scooters has been

included in the Council meeting document packet and staff has provided a recent article from the

Columbus Dispatch that discusses other municipalities approach to this topic. Ms. Lupton stated that the

proposed draft is preliminary in nature and staff will implement changes according to Council’s discussion

and recommendation. Mr. Justin Dreier, the Clerk of Council, provided a brief overview of the proposed

code. The code draft includes revisions to Chapter 301, 331, 337, 371, and 373. Proposed changes include

regulating e-bicycles and powered scooters in a similar manner as bicycles, implementing a helmet

requirement for minors, and regulating e-bicycle and powered scooter usage on shared-use paths,

sidewalks, and speed limits related thereto.

Ms. Arnold stated the importance of community education regarding e-bicycles and powered scooters,

given that many residents may not understand the safety impacts of using such equipment. She noted

that an in-school program may be beneficial as well.

Mr. Harriman noted that the informational post on the Village’s social media platforms approximately one

month ago had significant engagement from residents and that responses were in favor of enacting e

bicycle and powered scooter regulations. Mr. Harriman stated his support for educating the public on

helmet safety as well, especially in regards to most bicycle helmets not being rated for e-bicycles or

powered scooters given the maximum speed difference.

Mr. Sintz stated that he is unsure if enforcing helmet regulations would increase pedestrian safety. He

acknowledged that, while helmet usage is important and valuable to the operator’s safety, he would not be

800 Village Boulevard | P.O. Box 167 | Plain City, OH 43064 | 614-873-3527 | plaincityohio.gov

in favor of requiring helmet usage in the form of a law. However, Mr. Sintz noted that speed limits for e

bicycles and powered scooters should be implemented and enforced. At the inquiry of Ms. Arnold, it was

confirmed by Mr. Dreier that some municipalities have implemented recommendatory guidelines for

helmet usage, rather than requiring it through a law. Ms. Arnold stated that a community education

campaign about helmet safety and usage may be more beneficial than enacting a law requiring such

usage. Mr. Sintz stated his support for Ms. Arnold’s suggestion. Chief McKee, at the inquiry of Council,

stated that enacting a helmet requirement would be difficult to enforce. He noted that the parent

responsibility clause within the proposed code has its merits but that the overall helmet requirement may

not be effective from an enforcement standpoint. Chief McKee stated his support for community

education and awareness and added that several police officers are certified for bicycle patrol and they

may be able to conduct educational school programs and/or adult classes to increase awareness. Chief

McKee acknowledged that such programs do require significant staff time.

Mr. Lewis stated that speed limits for e-bicycles and powered scooters may be difficult to enforce. Mr.

Harriman noted that it is important to implement certain regulations so that the Police Department is able

to address egregious public safety violations.

Ms. Arnold stated her support for community education options and suggested that Memorial Health may

be able to be a partner with the Village and assist in administering in-school educational programs and a

formal “ “Safety Town” program for the summer season. Ms. Arnold stated that encouraging certain

guidelines may be more feasible than requiring such actions through law.

Council, by unanimous consent, agreed that an e-bicycle and powered scooter registration system is not

desired.

Mr. Eudaily stated his support for regulating where e-bicycles and powered scooters may travel, as is

included in the proposed code. Ms. Arnold stated her support for implementing speed limit requirements

and regulations regarding e-bicycle and powered scooter usage on shared-use paths. Mr. Lewis noted that

such regulations would be difficult to enforce. Ms. Arnold stated her agreement but encouraged

regulations to be implemented to allow enforcement where feasible. Additional discussion took place

regarding appropriate location usage of e-bicycles and powered scooters. Ms. Arnold stated that she is

confident in the Police Department’s ability to use officer discretion for enforcement and noted that it is

her firm belief that the Police Department has the public’s best interests in mind. Chief McKee thanked Ms.

Arnold for the kind words.

Mr. Terry recommended revising the verbiage regarding signage prohibiting e-bicycle usage on shared

use paths in Section 373.11 such that excessive signage is not required to be installed on various pathways.

Mr. Terry encouraged staff to revise the proposed code according to tonight’s discussion and remove

regulations that staff believes are not necessary and present such revised code draft to Council

accordingly. Ms. Arnold suggested including a brief summary of the proposed code as well.

Ms. Lupton thanked Council for the information and stated that a similar topic that staff is beginning to

research is under-speed and low-speed vehicles, also known as golf carts. Currently, the Village only

regulates under-speed vehicles but regulations regarding low-speed vehicles should be reviewed for

applicability and necessity. Such code draft would be a separate project than the e-bicycle and powered

scooter topic. Council thanked Ms. Lupton for the information.

800 Village Boulevard | P.O. Box 167 | Plain City, OH 43064 | 614-873-3527 | plaincityohio.gov

Unfinished Business: None.

New Business:

1st Reading Ordinance 37-2026: Establishing a Temporary Moratorium on Data Center Development Within

1st Reading Resolution 31-2026: Declaring the Intersections of Madison Way and Abby Drive and Madison

1st Reading Resolution 32-2026: Amending Resolution 22-2026 and the Contract with Chemcote Inc for

MEMORANDUM

To: Members of the Village of Plain City Council

From: Haley Lupton, Village Administrator

Date: June 9, 2026

RE: Ordinance 34-2026: Amending the Codified Ordinances Through the Enactment of

Chapter 1178, Architectural and Site Design Standards for Residential Development,

of Part Eleven of the Planning and Zoning Code

Background:

Village staff has conducted research and, in conjunction with the Planning and Zoning

Commission, have formulated a draft of the proposed code for the enactment of Chapter 1178.

The enactment of Chapter 1178 would be a new addition to the Planning and Zoning Code. Chapter

1178, titled “Architectural and Site Design Standards for Residential Development”, will set forth

the minimum standards for the architectural design of new residential developments within the

Village of Plain City. Proposed provisions of Chapter 1178 include regulations related to the

development site layout, structure materials, elevation requirements, structure orientation, park

amenities, and landscaping. This enactment aligns with similarly enacted revisions to the

Planning and Zoning Code in the form of Chapter 1177, “Architectural Design Standards for

Commercial or Multi-Family Development, through the passage of Ordinance 48-2025. The

proposed enactment is attached to Ordinance 34-2026 in the form of Exhibit A.

The Planning and Zoning Commission’s review of the proposed code amendment took place

during the meeting on May 20, 2026. The Commission heard comments from staff during its

review. Upon review and discussion, the Commission voted to recommend approval of the

proposed code amendment.

Objective:

To approve the enactment of Chapter 1178 of the Village of Plain City Codified Ordinances.

800 Village Boulevard | P.O. Box 167 | Plain City, OH 43064 | 614-873-3527 | plaincityohio.gov

Recommendation:

Staff supports and recommends the passage of

ORDINANCE NO. 34-2026

AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE CODIFIED ORDINANCES THROUGH THE

ENACTMENT OF CHAPTER 1178, ARCHITECTURAL AND SITE DESIGN

STANDARDS FOR RESIDENTIAL DEVELOPMENT, OF PART ELEVEN OF THE

PLANNING AND ZONING CODE

WHEREAS, the Village of Plain City Codified Ordinances contains Title Seven, “Additional

Zoning Regulations”, within Part Eleven of the Planning and Zoning Code which sets forth

regulations regarding various topics and subjects as it relates to the Zoning Code and

administration thereof; and

WHEREAS, upon the completion of thorough research and evaluation, it has been

recommended to this Village Council that Chapter 1178, titled “Architectural and Site Design

Standards for Residential Development”, be enacted and for such Chapter to establish

zoning regulations regarding the minimum standards for the architectural design for new

residential developments within the Village of Plain City; and

WHEREAS, the enactment of Chapter 1178 has been recommended to this Village Council in

order to promote the public health, safety, convenience, comfort, prosperity, and general

welfare of the residents of the Village of Plain City, and to further guide and facilitate the

development and improvement of subdivided land within the Village of Plain City; and

WHEREAS, following its duly advertised public hearing, the Planning and Zoning

Commission reviewed the proposed amendment during its meeting on May 20, 2026, and

upon review and discussion, the Planning and Zoning Commission voted to recommend that

the Village Council approve the proposed amendment; and

WHEREAS, a public hearing before the Village Council to consider the amendment to the

Planning and Zoning Code, being duly advertised according to the requirements of the Plain

City Codified Ordinances, has occurred during the meeting on July 13, 2026; and

WHEREAS, the Village Council concurs with the recommendation and determination of

staff and the Planning and Zoning Commission to enact Chapter 1178 of Part Eleven of the

Planning and Zoning Code and has determined the enactment to be in the best interests of

the Village of Plain City; and

WHEREAS, t the Village Council has determined the need to enact Chapter 1178 of Part Eleven

of the Planning and Zoning Code, in accordance with Exhibit A, which is attached hereto and

incorporated herein.

NOW THEREFORE BE IT ORDAINED by the Council of the Village of Plain City, Ohio, a majority

of the members elected or appointed thereto concurring, as follows:

Section 1. The Village Council hereby amends the Codified Ordinances through the

enactment of Chapter 1178 of the Planning and Zoning Code, as presented, which said

amendment is attached hereto and incorporated herein as Exhibit A.

Section 2. It is found that all formal actions of this Council concerning and relating to the

passage of this Ordinance were adopted in an open meeting of this Council and that all

deliberations of this Council that resulted in this formal action were in meetings open to the

public in compliance with all legal requirements, including Section 121.22 of the Ohio

Revised Code.

Section 3. This Ordinance shall be effective from and after the earliest period provided by

law.

ORDINANCE 34-2026 EXHIBIT A

CHAPTER 1178

Architectural and Site Design Standards for Residential Development

1178.01 Purpose and Intent.

1178.02 Applicability.

1178.03 Development Layout Standards.

1178.04 Architectural Character and Exterior Materials.

1178.05 Park and Open Space Requirements.

1178.06 Landscaping Features.

1178.07 Fencing.

1178.08 Utilities and Service Area Screening.

1178.09 Mailbox Clusters.

1178.10 Stormwater Design Standards.

1178.11 Street Lighting.

1178.12 Development Submittal Requirements.

1178.13 Waivers.

1178.01 PURPOSE AND INTENT.

The purpose and intent of this Section is to preserve and reinforce the community’s

traditional small-town rural character while promoting high-quality, durable residential

construction. These standards are intended to ensure that new development is

compatible with existing neighborhoods and surrounding agricultural areas, and to

encourage pedestrian-oriented design patterns reflective of the community’s historic

rural form. The Section further seeks to integrate functional and visually attractive

stormwater management practices into site design, while minimizing visual clutter

associated with utilities and vehicular infrastructure.

1178.02 APPLICABILITY.

These standards shall apply to:

(a) All new residential subdivisions of five (5) or more lots;

(b) All planned unit developments containing residential dwellings;

(c) All multi-family residential developments;

(d) Any phased residential development.

(e) Single-lot infill development in established subdivisions shall comply with Sections

1178.04 and 1178.06 where feasible as determined by the Zoning Administrator.

1178.03 DEVELOPMENT LAYOUT STANDARDS.

(a) Neighborhood Pattern and Street Layout.

(1) Residential subdivisions shall avoid rigid or urban-style block grid street

patterns. Development shall incorporate a curvilinear street layout reflective

of rural roadways, including:

A. Streets with gentle curves and bends;

B. Occasional long straightaways consistent with rural character;

C. Variations in alignment that respond to natural topography, tree

lines, drainageways, and existing landscape features.

(2) Street layouts shall be designed to:

A. Preserve significant natural features;

B. Create visual interest and varied streetscapes;

C. Reduce monotony associated with repetitive grid patterns.

(3) Cul-de-sacs shall be minimized and permitted only where topography,

environmental constraints, or adjoining parcel configuration prevents

connectivity.

(b) Dwelling Orientation.

(1) The rear façade of a dwelling shall not face:

A. A public street; or

B. Public open space, parkland, or trail corridors.

(2) All principal structures shall orient their primary façade and entrance toward

a public street or common open space.

(3) Corner lots shall provide enhanced architectural treatment on both street

facing elevations.

1178.04 ARCHITECTURAL CHARACTER AND EXTERIOR MATERIALS.

Rural Small-Town Character. Residential structures shall reflect traditional rural

architectural styles common to small Midwestern and agricultural communities, including

pitched roofs, defined entries, and vertically proportioned windows.

(a) Variation. No more than two (2) identical front elevations shall be constructed

within four (4) consecutive lots.

(1) The Municipality encourages developments with attached units or four (4) or

more, a variation of elevation among the front facades.

(b) Exterior Materials.

(1) Primary Front Facing Elevations. (Corner lots have two front-facing

elevations.) Permitted Primary Materials. Primary exterior materials shall

consist of at least one (1) of the following:

A. Brick

B. Natural or manufactured stone

C. Fiber cement siding (e.g., Hardie board)

D. Engineered wood siding

(2) Prohibited Materials.

A. Vinyl siding is prohibited on primary façades.

B. Corrugated metal panels are prohibited on residential dwellings

(except agricultural accessory buildings).

(3) Material Continuity.

A. Primary materials shall extend a minimum of two (2) feet around

side elevations.

(c) Dwelling Entry Features. All dwellings shall include a defined entry feature, such as:

(1) Covered Porch

A. Covered porches shall have a minimum depth of four (4) feet.

(2) Portico

(3) Overhang

(4) Stoops with roof projection

(5) Front doors shall face the public street.

(d) Windows and Openings.

(1) All elevations facing the public right-of-way or shared/public open space

shall contain windows.

(2) In cases where the floor plan results in an exterior wall without windows,

faux windows shall be provided to maintain visual consistency.

(3) Blank wall segments exceeding twenty (20) feet in length are prohibited.

(e) Rooflines. Rooflines shall incorporate variation through gables, dormers, changes

in pitch, or projections.

(f) Garages.

(1) Garage doors shall not dominate the primary façade.

(2) Garage projections beyond the primary façade shall not exceed twelve (12)

feet.

1178.05 PARK AND OPEN SPACE REQUIREMENTS.

The purpose of this Section is to ensure that residential developments incorporate usable,

accessible, and well-designed park and open space areas that support recreation, social

interaction, and overall quality of life. These spaces shall include amenities that serve a

range of age groups and contribute to the visual and functional character of the

development.

(a) Applicability. These standards shall apply to all residential developments that

include required or voluntary common open space, including subdivisions, planned

unit developments, and mixed residential communities.

(b) General Requirements.

(1) Accessibility.

A. All park and open space areas shall be accessible to residents via

sidewalks, trails, or pedestrian connections.

B. Spaces shall be designed to be visible and easily identifiable from

public or common areas.

(2) Distribution.

A. Open spaces shall be distributed throughout the development to

serve the greatest number of residents.

B. Larger developments should include multiple open space areas

rather than a single centralized space where feasible.

(3) Design Integration.

A. Parks and open spaces shall be integrated with surrounding

development, streets, and pedestrian networks.

B. Amenities shall be arranged to encourage active and passive use

while maintaining safety and visibility.

(c) Minimum Amenity Requirements. All required park and open space areas shall

include, at a minimum, the following amenities unless otherwise approved by the

Planning and Zoning Commission:

(1) Seating.

A. Benches or other seating elements shall be provided.

B. Minimum standard: One (1) bench per 5,000 square feet of park

area, with at least two (2) benches required in any single park space.

C. Seating shall be located along walking paths, near playgrounds, and

in shaded or scenic areas.

(2) Pedestrian Paths. Walking paths or trails shall be provided within or

connected to open space areas. Paths shall:

A. Be a minimum of five (5) feet in width for walking paths and a

minimum of eight (8) feet for shared/multi-use paths;

B. Provide looped or connected circulation where feasible;

C. Connect to the broader pedestrian network.

(3) Play Areas.

A. Play areas shall be centrally located or evenly distributed

throughout the development to ensure that no dwelling unit is more

than 1,320 feet from a play area in developments over 200 units.

i. Play equipment shall include a mix of play equipment

appropriate for intended age groups;

ii. Shall comply with applicable safety standards (e.g., ASTM,

CPSC);

iii. At least one play area per development shall be ADA

accessible.

B. Seating for caregivers shall be located adjacent to play areas.

C. Exemption. Developments dedicated to individuals age 55 years and

above shall not be required to provide play areas.

D. The Planning and Zoning Commission may approve a reduction in

required play area where an existing public park or playground

(connected by safe pedestrian access) is within 1,320 feet of

dwelling units. In such cases, required play areas may be reduced by

up to 50%.

1178.06 LANDSCAPING FEATURES.

The purpose of this Section is to establish minimum landscaping standards that enhance

the aesthetic quality of residential development, promote environmental sustainability,

provide visual buffering, and create cohesive, attractive neighborhoods. Landscaping

shall be designed to complement architectural features, define spaces, and contribute to

the overall character of the development.

(a) General Landscaping Requirements.

(1) Landscape Plan Required. A comprehensive landscape plan shall be

submitted for approval as part of the development application. The plan

shall include:

A. Plant species, sizes, and quantities;

B. Irrigation methods (if applicable);

C. Mulching and groundcover details;

D. Maintenance provisions.

(2) Plant Material Standards.

A. Native or adaptive species are strongly encouraged.

B. Plant materials shall be hardy to the local climate and suitable for

site conditions.

(3) Installation Standards.

A. Landscaping shall be installed prior to issuance of a final certificate

of occupancy, or a financial guarantee shall be provided.

B. All landscaped areas shall be mulched and maintained in a healthy

growing condition.

(4) Maintenance.

A. Property owners or homeowners’ associations shall be responsible

for ongoing maintenance.

B. Dead or diseased plant materials shall be replaced within one

growing season.

(b) Foundation Landscaping (Individual Lots).

(1) Applicability. Foundation landscaping shall be required along the front

façade of all principal residential structures.

(2) Minimum Requirements.

A. A continuous planting bed shall be provided along at least 60% of

the front building façade, excluding driveways and walkways.

B. Plantings shall include a combination of:

i. Shrubs (minimum 2-gallon size at installation);

ii. Perennials and/or ornamental grasses.

(3) Design Standards.

A. Plantings shall be arranged to provide visual interest and seasonal

variation.

B. Foundation plantings shall not obstruct primary entrances,

windows, or required sight lines.

C. Use of evergreen shrubs is encouraged to provide year-round

structure.

(c) Entryway and Signage Landscaping.

(1) Development Entrances. Primary entrances shall include enhanced

landscaping to create a recognizable gateway feature. Landscaping shall

include:

A. A mix of canopy trees, ornamental trees, shrubs, and groundcover;

B. Decorative elements such as berms, stone walls, or accent features

(where applicable).

C. Wood fencing shall be incorporated in the design of development

entrances and along major arterial and collector roads.

(2) Signage Areas. All development signage shall be integrated into a

landscaped setting.

A. A planting bed shall be provided around the base of all signs, with:

i. A minimum width of five (5) feet;

ii. Layered plantings including low shrubs, ornamental grasses,

and seasonal color.

(d) Common Areas and Open Space Landscaping. Landscaping shall be provided in all

common areas, including parks, green spaces, stormwater facilities, and trail

corridors.

(1) Minimum Requirements.

A. A mix of canopy trees, understory trees, shrubs, and groundcover

shall be provided.

B. Landscaping shall be designed to:

i. Frame open spaces.

ii. Provide shade.

iii. Enhance usability and comfort.

(2) Parks and Gathering Areas.

A. Shade trees shall be provided at a minimum ratio of one (1) tree per

2,500 square feet of active use area.

B. Landscaping shall complement recreational features such as

seating areas, playgrounds, and plazas.

(3) Trails and Pedestrian Corridors.

A. Landscaping shall be provided along trails to define the corridor and

enhance user experience.

B. Native grasses and low-maintenance plantings are encouraged.

C. Trees shall be spaced to provide intermittent shade without

obstructing visibility or safety.

(4) Streetscape Landscaping. Street tree requirements shall comply with

Chapter 1187.

(e) Flexibility and Alternatives. The reviewing authority may approve alternative

landscaping designs that meet or exceed the intent of this Section, particularly

where innovative, sustainable, or site-sensitive solutions are proposed.

1178.07 FENCING.

Fences within a subdivision shall generally maintain consistency in materials, height, and

colors.

(a) Fencing in front yards are recommended to be prohibited within the proposed

zoning development text.

(b) Corner lots which have two (2) front yards shall comply with Section 1183.12.

(c) Chain-link fencing is prohibited.

1178.08 UTILITIES AND SERVICE AREA SCREENING.

(a) Utility boxes, transformers, backflow preventers, and mechanical equipment shall

be screened by:

(1) Evergreen landscaping; or

(2) Architectural enclosures consistent with principal structures.

(3) HVAC units shall not be located between the front façade and the street.

1178.09 MAILBOX CLUSTERS.

(a) Cluster mailbox structures shall complement the architectural character of the

development.

(b) Materials shall be consistent with principal building materials.

(c) Landscaping shall be installed around the base of mailbox structures and shall meet

the requirements of Section 1178.06.

1178.10 STORMWATER DESIGN STANDARDS.

(a) Detention and retention basins shall be integrated into the overall design of the

development and shall be located and designed to function as both stormwater

management facilities and visual amenities.

(b) Detention basins shall incorporate the following design elements:

(1) Water features such as fountains or aeration systems, where appropriate, to

enhance aesthetics and water quality;

(2) Gently sloping side slopes to promote safety and ease of maintenance;

(3) Naturalized edges, including large stone or similar materials along the

water’s edge where appropriate;

(4) Native grasses, plantings, or other vegetation to enhance water quality and

visual appearance;

(5) Decorative landscaping to integrate the basin into the surrounding

development;

(6) Pedestrian amenities such as walking paths, seating areas, or overlooks,

where feasible and appropriate.

(c) All mechanical and operational components, including outlet structures, control

devices, and associated equipment, shall be screened from view from public

streets and adjacent properties using landscaping, berms, or other approved

methods.

(d) Low Impact Development (LID) techniques, including but not limited to bioswales,

rain gardens, and permeable paving, are encouraged and may be required where

determined appropriate by the reviewing authority to meet stormwater

management objectives.

1178.11 STREET LIGHTING.

The purpose of this Section is to ensure adequate illumination of streets and pedestrian

areas within residential developments to promote safety, visibility, and neighborhood

character.

(a) Street lighting shall be required for all public and private streets within residential

subdivisions and planned developments.

(b) Streetlights shall be installed at the following locations:

(1) At all street intersections.

(2) At all designated mid-block pedestrian crossings.

(3) Streetlights shall be spaced at intervals not exceeding 200 feet on opposite

sides of the street, unless an alternative spacing is approved based on a

photometric plan demonstrating equivalent or greater illumination.

(4) Additional fixtures may be required where necessary to address curves,

changes in grade, or other site-specific conditions.

(c) Lighting fixtures style and design shall be submitted with lighting plans and subject

to approval.

1178.12 DEVELOPMENT SUBMITTAL REQUIREMENTS.

(a) All residential development submittals, in addition to application requirements

within the Zoning Code, shall include:

(1) Streets layout;

(2) Architectural elevations of all models from all sides that will be offered;

(3) Exterior material specifications for all structures;

(4) Landscaping plan for the entire development including individual lots;

(5) Stormwater design plan;

(6) Lighting plan.

1178.13 WAIVERS.

The Planning and Zoning Commission may grant minor waivers where strict application

would create undue hardship, provided the overall intent and rural character objectives of

this Section are maintained.

MEMORANDUM

To: Members of the Village of Plain City Council

From: Haley Lupton, Village Administrator

Date: June 9, 2026

RE: Ordinance 35-2026: Amending the Codified Ordinances, Removal of Chapter 1182,

Blight Control, of Part Eleven of the Planning and Zoning Code

Background:

Village staff has conducted research and, in conjunction with the Planning and Zoning

Commission, have recommended the removal of Chapter 1182 of the Planning and Zoning Code.

Chapter 1182, titled “Blight Control”, sets forth minimum standards regarding the exterior

maintenance, condition, and appearance of structures and premises within the Village of Plain

City. The proposed removal is in conjunction with the enactment of Chapter 1325, titled “Exterior

Property Maintenance”, further detailed through the passage of Ordinance 17-2026. The

enactment of Chapter 1325 sets forth comprehensive provisions regarding the exterior

maintenance of all structures and premises within the Village. The proposed amendment is

attached to Ordinance 35-2026 in the form of Exhibit A.

The Planning and Zoning Commission’s review of the proposed code removal took place during

the meeting on May 20, 2026. The Commission heard comments from staff during its review.

Upon review and discussion, the Commission voted to recommend the removal of Chapter 1182.

Objective:

To approve the removal of Chapter 1182 of the Village of Plain City Codified Ordinances.

Recommendation:

Staff supports and recommends the passage of Ordinance 35-2026 at the second reading on

August 10, 2026. The removal of Chapter 1182 increases consistency and clarity within the

Planning and Zoning Code pursuant to the enactment and implementation of Chapter 1325.

800 Village Boulevard | P.O. Box 167 | Plain City, OH 43064 | 614-873-3527 | plaincityohio.gov

ORDINANCE NO. 35-2026

AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE CODIFIED ORDINANCES, REMOVAL OF

CHAPTER 1182, BLIGHT CONTROL, OF PART ELEVEN OF THE PLANNING AND

ZONING CODE

WHEREAS, the Village of Plain City Codified Ordinances Chapter 1182 sets forth various

regulations and minimum standards for the exterior maintenance, condition, and

appearance of structures and premises within the Village of Plain City; and

recommended to this Village Council that Chapter 1182 be removed in accordance with the

through the passage of Ordinance 17-2026; and

the Village Council approve the proposed amendment; and

Planning and Zoning Code, being duly advertised according to the requirements of the Plain

City Codified Ordinances, has occurred during the meeting on July 13, 2026; and

WHEREAS, the Village Council concurs with the recommendation and determination of

staff and the Planning and Zoning Commission to remove Chapter 1182 of the Planning and

Zoning Code and has determined the amendment to be in the best interests of the Village

of Plain City; and

WHEREAS, the Village Council has determined the need to remove Chapter 1182, in

accordance with Exhibit A, which is attached hereto and incorporated herein.

passage of this Ordinance were adopted in an open meeting of this Council and that all

deliberations of this Council that resulted in this formal action were in meetings open to the

public in compliance with all legal requirements, including Section 121.22 of the Ohio

Revised Code.

ORDINANCE 35-2026 EXHIBIT A

CHAPTER 1182

Blight Control Repealed

1182.01 Definitions.

1182.02 Purpose.

1182.03 Maintenance of buildings and fences.

1182.04 Maintenance of premises.

1182.05 Reserved.

1182.06 Removal of miscellaneous debris.

1182.07 Steps, walks and driveways.

1182.08 Notices of violation; noncompliance; remedies of Village.

1182.09 Correction of violations by Village; costs; liens.

1182.10 Reports of violations.

1182.11 Appeals

1182.99 Penalty.

CROSS REFERENCES

Removal of unsafe structures  – see Ohio R.C. 715.26(B), 715.261

Removal of noxious weeds or litter  – see Ohio R.C. 731.51 et seq.

Public nuisances – see Ohio R.C. Ch. 3767

Duty to keep sidewalks in repair and clean  – see Gen. Off. 521.06

1182.01 DEFINITIONS.

See Section 1137.02.

1182.02 PURPOSE.

The purpose of this Chapter is to protect the public health, safety and general welfare by

establishing minimum standards governing the exterior maintenance, condition and

appearance of structures and premises; to prevent blighting problems and enhance

property values; to fix responsibilities and duties upon owners and occupants of

structures with respect to sanitation, repair and maintenance; to authorize and establish

procedures for the inspection of premises; and to provide for penalties for violations of

this Chapter. This Blight Control Chapter shall apply to all structures and premises within

the Village.

1182.03 MAINTENANCE OF BUILDINGS AND FENCES.

(a) All buildings and structures shall comply with the applicable provisions of Part 13 -

Building Code, of the Village Codified Ordinances. The exterior and condition of all

buildings and structures on any premises shall be maintained so that the

appearance thereof reflects a level of maintenance in keeping with the standards

of the Village as set forth in this Chapter and so as to avoid blighting influences

and hazards to health and safety.

(b) All buildings on any property that are a blighting influence shall be removed or

rehabilitated by the owner. All buildings which are to remain shall provide

weather-proofed usable space and shall not harbor termites or other vermin, such

as rats and mice or otherwise, directly or indirectly, threaten the health and safety

of the general public.

(c) Fences, retaining walls or similar structures shall comply with the requirements of

Sections 1183.02 and 1183.12, shall be anchored firmly in the ground, shall be

constructed in a workmanlike manner, shall be maintained so that such fences,

retaining walls or similar structures shall always be in a state of good structural

repair and shall not constitute a threat to the health and safety of the general

public and/or a blighting influence. Finish coatings shall be maintained and free

from peeling paint. In the alternative, such fences, retaining walls or similar

structures shall be removed or replaced by the owner. If necessary, in such cases

where the structural repair is in question, the Village Engineer or the County

Building Department, whichever may be applicable, will be notified to provide a

professional opinion. Any opinion of the Village Engineer or County Building

Department shall be binding.

1182.04 MAINTENANCE OF PREMISES.

(a) The owner, lessee, agent or tenant of the owner having charge of the premises

shall keep the exterior of all buildings and other structures on the premises,

including, but not limited to, walls, gutters, downspouts, roofs, cornices,

chimneys, drains, towers, porches, landings, fire escapes, stairs, store fronts,

signs, windows, doors, awnings and marquees, in good repair, and all surfaces

thereof shall be kept protected for the purpose of preservation and avoiding a

blighting influence to adjoining properties. All surfaces shall be maintained free of

broken glass, loose shingles, crumbling stone or brick or other conditions

reflective of deterioration or inadequate maintenance, to the end that the

property itself may be preserved safely, fire hazards eliminated and adjoining

properties and neighborhoods protected from blighting influences.

(b) All premises shall be appropriately maintained, and lawns, hedges, bushes, trees

and other vegetation shall be kept trimmed and from becoming overgrown as

provided for in Chapters 1183 and 1186, where exposed to public view or where

such vegetation may constitute a blighting influence or obstruct a public right of

way. However, this subsection shall not preclude the maintenance of undeveloped

or underdeveloped land in its natural state.

(c) All canopies, marquees, signs, awnings, exterior stairways, fire escapes,

standpipes, exhaust ducts, porches, balconies and similar overhanging or

protruding extensions, where exposed to public view, shall be maintained in good

condition and shall not show evidence of weathering, rotting, ripping, tearing or

other holes or breaks.

(d) No furniture (except lawn furniture which is designed for exclusive outdoor use

and in good repair), mattresses, household furnishings, rugs, appliances, unused

posts, abandoned, dilapidated or junk vehicles or vehicle parts, tires, discarded or

abandoned machinery, equipment, construction equipment, construction

material, brush, broken glass, railroad ties, telephone poles, roots, stumps, fallen

trees, filth, garbage, trash, drums, and debris shall be placed or stored in any yard

area, outdoor living area, porch, or carport, within the Village.

(e) Construction dumpsters may not remain on a property for more than ninety (90)

consecutive days. The construction dumpster must be removed within thirty (30)

days of completion or cessation of construction. If necessary, a request for an

extension, not to exceed ninety (90) days per extension, may be made by the

owner of the property to the Village Zoning Inspector.

(f) Portable storage units may not remain on a property for more than thirty (30)

consecutive days.

(1) No more than one (1) portable storage unit on a lot at any given time without

the express permission of the Village Zoning Inspector who may approve

one (1) additional portable storage unit upon a showing of good cause.

(2) No storage unit can obstruct traffic visibility when placed on a property.

(3) During construction, a portable storage unit may be used to store on site

tools. The portable storage unit must be removed within fifteen (15) days of

cessation of construction.

(4) As used in this Section, "cessation" means a temporary stop of

construction for thirty (30) days or more, or final stopping of all

construction action.

(5) Upon request and a showing of good cause, the Village Zoning Inspector

may grant a thirty (30) day extension for the placement of a portable

storage unit or additional units.

(g) As used in this Section, "junk vehicle" means a motor vehicle, boat or trailer that

meets any one of the following criteria:

(1) Not readily operable on the roadways of Ohio under Ohio R.C. 4513 or

waterways under Ohio R.C. 1547;

(2) Extensively damaged, including, but not limited to, any of the following:

missing wheels, tires, engine, or transmission, missing or broken glass,

missing body panels;

(3) Unregistered in the State of Ohio or any other state;

(h) Automotive repair, storage, and restoration is prohibited in all residential zoning

districts, except for work on a personal vehicle of the resident.

(i) No laundry, including but not limited to: garments, towels, bedding, and clothing

shall be hung on front porches or balconies.

1182.05 RESERVED.

1182.06 REMOVAL OF MISCELLANEOUS DEBRIS.

All yards, courts or lots shall be kept free of debris which may cause a fire hazard or act as

a breeding place for vermin or insects, unsightly material not appropriate to the area

and/or which have a blighting influence on the area. All trash or debris shall be placed into

trash containers suitable for such use. All trash containers shall be stored out of sight

from street right-of-way.

1182.07 STEPS, WALKS AND DRIVEWAYS.

All steps, paths, walkways, and off-street parking, including spaces, parking lots, parking

area, drives, driveways, aisles and circulation drives shall be constructed to promote

safety, kept free from deterioration and blighting influences, and maintained free of chuck

holes/pot holes, litter, glass, nails or other dangerous materials. If any such area, by

reason of its state of repair, violates this Section, it shall be repaired or replaced within

thirty (30) days. Hazards and unsanitary conditions shall be eliminated immediately. All

walks which exist within the public right of way which are now paved shall be maintained in

accordance with Chapter 905 of the Codified Ordinances.

1182.08 NOTICES OF VIOLATION; NONCOMPLIANCE; REMEDIES OF VILLAGE.

(a) When any premises is in violation of this Chapter, the Village Zoning Inspector or

his/her designee shall issue a notice of violation to the owner, lessee, agent or

tenant of the owner having charge of the premises, by certified mail, return receipt

requested, or by personal service by a Municipal employee designated by the Village

Zoning Inspector to perform such service. This notice of violation shall specify the

violation committed and contain an order to conform to this Chapter within a

certain number of days, not to exceed one-hundred and eighty (180) days. In the

event that weather or other unforeseen circumstances prevents correcting or

abating the violation, the Village Zoning Inspector may extend the time set forth

herein.

(b) If the owner does not comply with the order contained in the notice of violation,

then the Village Zoning Inspector or his/her designee shall have the provisions of

this Chapter enforced and shall cause the violation to be corrected. Upon the

approval of Village Council, the Village Administrator may employ the necessary

labor and equipment to perform such task, together with any cleanup work

required. All expenses incurred shall, when approved by Council, be paid out of the

General Fund of the Village.

(c) In addition, the owner shall be liable for the penalties provided in this Chapter.

1182.09 CORRECTION OF VIOLATIONS BY VILLAGE; COSTS; LIENS.

(a) Whenever any violation of this Chapter is corrected by the Village, having given

prior notice as set forth in Section 1182.08 and upon completion of the work, the

Village Administrator shall invoice the owner, lessee, agent or tenant of the owner

having charge of the premises in violation, at the last known address of such

person and require payment of the charges incurred within five (5) days. The

invoiced costs of the corrective work required shall be for the actual costs

incurred by the Village, including the following:

(1) Administration and supervision;

(2) Transportation of equipment;

(3) Equipment rental;

(4) Equipment operator;

(5) Incidental labor;

(6) Materials provided; and

(7) Legal fees.

(b) If such charges are not paid within thirty (30) days after receipt of the notice, then

the Village Fiscal Officer shall make a written notice to the County Auditor of its

action under this Chapter, with a statement of the approved charges, the amount

paid for the performing of labor, the fees of the officers who made the service of

the notice and return and a proper description of the premises by legal

description or permanent parcel number. Such amounts, as approved by the

Village Council, shall be entered upon the tax duplicate, shall be a lien upon such

lands from the date of the entry and shall be collected as other taxes and returned

to the Village with the General Fund.

1182.10 REPORTS OF VIOLATIONS.

Any resident or employee of the Village may report violations of this Chapter to the Village

Zoning Inspector for appropriate actions.

1182.11 APPEALS.

(a) Any person directly affected by a decision of the Village Zoning Inspector or a

notice of order issued under this Code Section shall have the right to appeal to the

Board of Zoning Appeals within twenty (20) days of the date of the notice or order

by filing a written notice with the Village Zoning Inspector and the Board of Zoning

Appeals.

(b) An application for appeal shall be based on a claim that the true intent of this Code

or the rules legally adopted hereunder have been incorrectly interpreted, the

provisions of the Code do not fully apply, the requirements of this Code are

adequately satisfied by other means, and/or the strict application of any

requirement of this Code would cause an undue and unnecessary hardship. The

cost for the application for appeal under this Section is listed under Section

1135.04(f) and is applicable for each offense for which an appeal is requested.

(c) The Board of Zoning Appeals shall hear such appeal in accordance with the

provisions of Section 1138.04 of the Codified Ordinances of the Village of Plain

City, Ohio.

1182.99 PENALTY.

(a) Whenever the person responsible under this Chapter with the maintenance of a

structure or premises fails to comply with any final order as provided in this

Chapter, the Village Solicitor may file an action seeking injunctive relief or any

other remedies as provided by law.

(b) Whoever violates or fails to comply with any of the provisions of this Chapter is

guilty of a minor misdemeanor and shall be fined not more than one hundred

dollars ($100.00) for each offense. A separate offense shall be deemed committed

each day during or on which a violation or noncompliance occurs or continues.

MEMORANDUM

To: Members of the Village of Plain City Council

From: Haley Lupton, Village Administrator

Date: June 9, 2026

RE: Ordinance 36-2026: Amending the Codified Ordinances, Chapter 1186, Nuisances, of

Part Eleven of the Planning and Zoning Code

Background:

Village staff has conducted research and, in conjunction with the Planning and Zoning

Commission, have formulated proposed revisions to Chapter 1186 of the Planning and Zoning

Code. Chapter 1186, titled “Nuisances”, sets forth the regulations in regards to the sanitation and

health conditions of premises within the Village. Additionally, nuisance abatement procedures

are included within the existing code. Due to the enactment and implementation of Chapter 1325,

titled “Exterior Property Maintenance”, numerous provisions of Chapter 1186 are no longer

needed. As such, the proposed amendment would remove unnecessary language and rename the

Chapter “Animals, Bees”. The proposed amendment is attached to Ordinance 36-2026 in the form

of Exhibit A. The language in Exhibit A will replace the current provisions of Chapter 1186 in its

entirety.

The Planning and Zoning Commission’s review of the proposed code amendment took place

during the meeting on May 20, 2026. The Commission heard comments from staff during its

review. Upon review and discussion, the Commission voted to recommend approval of the

proposed code amendment.

Objective:

To approve amendments to Chapter 1186 of the Village of Plain City Codified Ordinances.

Planning and Zoning Code pursuant to the enactment and implementation of Chapter 1325

NUISANCES, OF PART ELEVEN OF THE PLANNING AND ZONING CODE

in its entirety.

ORDINANCE 36-2026 EXHIBIT A

CHAPTER 1186

Animals, Bees

1186.01 Purpose.

1186.02 Farm animals.

1186.03 Dogs.

1186.04 Bees.

1186.01 PURPOSE.

The purpose of this Chapter is to establish reasonable regulations for the keeping of

animals, dogs, bees, and related accessory structures within the Village in order to protect

the public health, safety, and welfare; minimize nuisances and unsanitary conditions;

promote compatibility with surrounding properties and neighborhoods; ensure humane

and responsible animal keeping practices; and provide standards that balance the

interests of animal owners with the rights of nearby residents to the safe and peaceful use

and enjoyment of their property. These regulations are further intended to support

agricultural and hobby activities in appropriate settings while preventing conditions that

may create noise, odor, safety, sanitation, or other adverse impacts.

1186.02 FARM ANIMALS.

(a) Purpose. The regulations of this Section are established to permit the keeping of

farm animals in a manner that prevents nuisances to occupants of nearby

properties and prevents conditions that are unsanitary or unsafe.

(b) Chickens, Ducks, Rabbits and Similar Animals. The keeping of chickens, ducks,

rabbits and similar farm animals, and cages, coops and enclosures for the keeping

of such animals, shall be governed by the following regulations.

(1) In Residential Districts, the following regulations shall apply:

A. Number. No more than one such animal shall be kept on a parcel of

land for each 725 square feet of parcel or lot area. For a standard

residential lot of 8700 square feet, this regulation would permit no

more than a total of twelve (12) such animals.

B. Setbacks. The coops or cages housing such animals may not be

located in front yard or side yard areas. Cages, coops, or other

habitations shall be located as to comply with the required rear yard

setbacks of the parcel according to the development standards. No

animals shall be kept in front yard and/or side yards.

C. Prohibitions. No roosters, geese, or turkeys may be kept in a

Residential District except on a parcel that is at least one (1) acre in

area and only if the coop or cage housing the bird(s) is at least one

hundred (100) feet from all property lines. For parcels greater than

one (1) acre in area, one (1) additional such bird may be kept for each

24,000 square feet in excess of one (1) acre. No predatory birds may

be kept on any property under the regulations of this Section

except raptors for use in the sport of Falconry as permitted under

Section 1533.05 of the Ohio Revised Code.

D. Coops and Cages. All animals shall be provided with a covered,

predator-proof coop or cage or other shelter that is thoroughly

ventilated, designed to be easily accessed, maintained and cleaned,

and of sufficient size to permit free movement of the animals,

exclusive of areas used for storage of materials or vehicles. The

total area of all coops or cages on a lot shall not be greater than fifty

(50) square feet for up to twelve (12) animals. Coops and cages,

singly or in combination, shall not exceed fifteen (15) feet in height.

E. Enclosures and Fences. Chickens and other birds shall have access

to an outdoor enclosure adequately fenced or otherwise bounded to

contain the birds on the property and to prevent access by dogs and

other predators and providing at least ten (10) square feet of area

for each bird.

F. Accessory Structures - Compliance with development standards.

i. Enclosures, coops, and cages that are easily portable by one

or two persons without use of machine and without

disassembly and that do not require a foundation are

considered temporary and portable are exempt from the

provisions of Section 1183.05 Accessory Uses and Structures.

ii. Enclosure, coops, and cages not meeting the criteria specified

under section (i) above are considered permanent and shall

comply with the provisions of Section 1183.05 Accessory Uses

and Structures.

(2) In Non-Residential Districts. In zoning districts other than Residential

Districts, all Regulations applicable in Residential Districts shall apply.

(c) Goats, Pigs, Sheep, Horses, Cows, Llamas, Alpacas and Similar Animals. Goats,

pigs, sheep, horses, cows, llamas, alpacas, and similar animals are only permitted in

RU Rural Districts. (See Chapter 1145).

1186.03 DOGS.

(a) Prohibitions. No more than three (3) dogs may be housed outdoors in a Residential

District or Residential Use except on a parcel that is at least one (1) acre in area and

only if the kennel housing the dogs is at least fifty (50) feet from all property lines.

For parcels greater than one (1) acre in area, a maximum of one (1) additional such

dog may be kept for each 24,000 square feet in excess of one (1) acre. For purposes

of this section, "outdoors" means all areas that are not within the primary structure

on the property.

1186.04 BEES.

(a) Purpose. The regulations of this Section are intended to establish certain

requirements of sound beekeeping practices, which are intended to avoid

problems that may otherwise be associated with the keeping of bees in populated

areas.

(b) Notwithstanding compliance with the various requirements of this Section, a

beekeeper shall be prohibited from keeping in any colony or colonies in such a

manner or of such disposition as to cause any unhealthy condition, interfere with

the normal use and enjoyment of human or animal life of others or interfere with the

normal use and enjoyment of any public property or property of others.

(c) All honey bee colonies shall be registered with the Ohio Department of Agriculture.

Operators (beekeepers) granted a certificate of zoning compliance to operate an

apiary must maintain a valid annual apiary registration. Beekeepers must comply

with the Ohio Apiary Law as set forth in Ohio Revised Code Chapter 909.

(d) Bees. The keeping of bees, and associated beehives, shall be governed by the

following regulations:

(1) Residential Districts. In Residential Districts, the following regulations shall

apply:

A. Number. No lot of less than 8,000 square feet shall have more than

two (2) colonies. One additional colony is permitted per additional

3,200 square feet with a maximum of six (6) colonies per lot.

B. Location and Setbacks. No beehive shall be kept closer than fifteen

(15) feet to any lot line and twenty (20) feet to a dwelling or the

permitted placement of a dwelling on another parcel, and no bee

hive shall be kept in a front yard or side street yard. The front of any

beehive shall face away from the property line of the Residential

property closest to the beehive.

C. Fences and Hedges. A solid fence or dense hedge, known as a

"flyway barrier," at least six (6) feet in height, shall be placed along

the side of the beehive that contains the entrance to the hive, and

shall be located within five (5) feet of the colony and shall extend at

least ten (10) feet on either side of the hive. No such flyway barrier

shall be required if all beehives are located at least twenty-five (25)

feet from all property lines and for beehives that are located on

porches or balconies at least ten (10) feet above grade, except if

such porch or balcony is located less than five (5) feet from a

property line. All fences and hedges shall comply with Section

1183.12 of this Code.

D. Water Supply. A supply of fresh water shall be maintained in a

location readily accessible to all bee colonies on the site throughout

the day to prevent bees from congregating at neighboring

swimming pools or other sources of water on nearby properties.

that the number of beehives shall be limited to one (1) for each 1,000 square

Districts, all regulations applicable in Residential Districts shall apply except

Residential Districts. In zoning districts other than Residential

MEMORANDUM

To: Members of the Village of Plain City Council

From: Haley Lupton, Village Administrator

Date: July 23, 2026

RE: Ordinance 37-2026: Establishing a Temporary Moratorium on Data Center

Development Within the Corporate Limits of the Village of Plain City and Declaring

an Emergency

Background:

At the direction of the Village Council, and pursuant to the discussion that took place during the

July 13, 2026 Council meeting, legal counsel and staff has drafted legislation to enact a temporary

moratorium on any new applications related to the development of data centers within the

municipal limits of the Village of Plain City. The temporary moratorium has been proposed in

order to provide Council, staff, and legal counsel adequate time to research and determine

appropriate zoning regulations to implement in regards to the development of data centers. The

length of the temporary moratorium is for one (1) year and Council may, at their discretion, extend

or revoke the temporary moratorium with additional legislation.

Objective:

To approve legislation to enact a temporary moratorium on the development of data centers

within the Village of Plain City for a term of one (1) year.

Recommendation:

Staff supports and recommends the passage of Ordinance 37-2026. Staff requests the Village

Council’s consideration in waiving the second reading and declaring an emergency for Ordinance

37-2026 and approving said legislation during the meeting on August 5, 2026. The declaration of

emergency is to preserve the public peace, health, safety, and welfare of the Village of Plain City

and its residents due to the unknown impacts from a data center development within municipal

limits. Pursuant to the Plain City Charter Section 4.04 and Section 4.08, a waiver of the second

reading and declaration of emergency requires an affirmative vote of five (5) Councilmembers.

800 Village Boulevard | P.O. Box 167 | Plain City, OH 43064 | 614-873-3527 | plaincityohio.gov

ORDINANCE NO. 37-2026

AN ORDINANCE ESTABLISHING A TEMPORARY MORATORIUM ON DATA

CENTER DEVELOPMENT WITHIN THE CORPORATE LIMITS OF THE VILLAGE

OF PLAIN CITY AND DECLARING AN EMERGENCY

WHEREAS, pursuant to the Constitution of the State of Ohio and the Ohio Revised Code, the

Village of Plain City has the power and authority to regulate land use, zoning, and

development within the Village and adopt and enforce regulations to protect the public

health, safety, and general welfare of residents of the Village; and

WHEREAS, data centers may generate substantial noise, vibration, heat discharge,

electrical load, water discharge, stormwater impacts, and other environmental effects

depending on site-specific conditions; and

WHEREAS, the Village Council desires time to research, study, develop, and implement

regulations regarding the zoning and development of data centers; and

WHEREAS, the Village Council has determined that allowing new data center related

application or approvals to proceed before such an investigation and research is completed

would risk the establishment of uses that may adversely impact the public health, safety,

welfare of the residents of the Village; and

WHEREAS, it is the intent of the Village Council that this Ordinance function solely as a

temporary planning and zoning measure to allow the Village Council, staff, and legal counsel

to conduct a comprehensive investigation and research into the potential impacts

associated with data centers, and to appropriately implement regulations related thereto;

and

WHEREAS, the Village Council believes that such a moratorium on data center related

business and development is necessary to ensure the health, safety, and welfare of the

residents of the Village of Plain City and for such moratorium to be in effect for a term of

one (1) year, with the provisions that Council may extend or remove such moratorium at its

discretion.

NOW THEREFORE BE IT ORDAINED by the Council of the Village of Plain City, Ohio, a majority

of the members elected or appointed thereto concurring, as follows:

Section 1. Pursuant to the authority set forth in the Constitution of the State of Ohio and the

Ohio Revised Code, the Village of Plain City Council hereby establishes a temporary

moratorium for a term of one (1) year with respect to the acceptance of applications for

development that include any data centers.

Section 2. The Village Council may extend or remove the temporary moratorium by

legislative action, subject to the discretion of the Village Council as the legislative authority

of the Village of Plain City.

Section 3. The provisions of this Ordinance does not apply to any zoning applications and/or

development applications filed with the Village of Plain City prior to August 5, 2026.

Furthermore, the provisions of this Ordinance does not apply to land within the Village of

Plain City that contains a data center use as a permitted or conditional use within its current

zoning classification.

Section 4. This legislation is hereby declared to be an emergency measure necessary for

the immediate preservation of the public peace, health, safety, and welfare of the Village of

Plain City and its inhabitants, and further provided it receives the affirmative vote of five

members elected to Council, it shall take effect and be in force from and after the earliest

period provided by law.

Section 5. It is found that all formal actions of this Council concerning and relating to the

passage of this Ordinance were adopted in an open meeting of this Council and that all

deliberations of this Council that resulted in this formal action were in meetings open to the

public in compliance with all legal requirements, including Section 121.22 of the Ohio

Revised Code.

Section 6. This Ordinance shall be effective from and after the earliest period provided by

law.

Passed: , 2026

MEMORANDUM

To: Members of the Village of Plain City Council

From: Haley Lupton, Village Administrator

Date: July 6, 2026

RE: Resolution 25-2026: Authorizing and Directing the Village Administrator to Apply to

the Ohio Public Works Commission for Clean Ohio Green Space Conservation

Program Funds

Background:

Village staff has begun preparations to apply to the grant funding opportunity administered by the

Ohio Public Works Commission (“OPWC”) for the Clean Ohio Green Space Conservation Program.

The program provides up to 75% reimbursement assistance for local government subdivisions for

eligible land acquisition or improvement projects. Staff is preparing an application for financial

assistance to complete improvements to the Sugar Run Park. The Village did receive financial

assistance in 2025 to acquire the aforementioned property through the same OPWC grant

program. Staff believes this program is an excellent opportunity to request additional financial

assistance in order to complete the Sugar Run Improvement Project in a fiscally responsible and

efficient manner.

In addition to the above project, staff is preparing an application for financial assistance for the

Darby Land Acquisition Project. This project, if approved, would provide funding to purchase the

approximately 6.77 acres along the Big Darby Creek and adjacent to Smucker Park and place such

land in a state of conservation and available for public and recreational use.

Required by the OPWC, to be submitted with the applications, is a legislative resolution of

authorization from the Village Council that authorizes and directs the Village Administrator to

complete and submit an application for funding. As such, staff is requesting Council’s review and

approval of Resolution 25-2026 to be able to submit the aforementioned applications.

800 Village Boulevard | P.O. Box 167 | Plain City, OH 43064 | 614-873-3527 | plaincityohio.gov

Objective:

To authorize the Village Administrator to apply to the Clean Ohio Green Space Conservation

Program as administered by the OPWC in order to request funding for the Sugar Run

Improvement Project and the Darby Land Acquisition Project.

Recommendation:

Staff supports and recommends the passage of Resolution 25-2026 at the second reading on

August 10, 2026. The submittal of an application for financial assistance furthers the goals of

Council and, if said application is approved, will provide necessary funding to complete vital

improvements at the Sugar Run Park and protect and conserve land along the Big Darby Creek

through the Darby Land Acquisition Project.

800 Village Boulevard | P.O. Box 167 | Plain City, OH 43064 | 614-873-3527 | plaincityohio.gov

RESOLUTION NO. 25-2026

A RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING AND DIRECTING THE VILLAGE

ADMINISTRATOR TO APPLY TO THE OHIO PUBLIC WORKS COMMISSION FOR

CLEAN OHIO GREEN SPACE CONSERVATION PROGRAM FUNDS

WHEREAS, the Ohio Public Works Commission (“OPWC”) administers financial assistance

for environmental conservation projects through the Clean Ohio Green Space Conservation

Program; and

WHEREAS, the Village Council desires financial assistance through the Clean Ohio Green

Space Conservation Program to complete the Sugar Run Improvement Project and the

Darby Land Acquisition Project; and

WHEREAS, the Village Council has determined that the submission of an application for

financial assistance through the Clean Ohio Green Space Conservation Program is in the

best interests of the Village of Plain City and its residents.

NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED by the Council of the Village of Plain City, Ohio, a majority

of the members elected or appointed thereto concurring, as follows:

Section 1. The Village Council hereby authorizes and directs the Village Administrator to

apply to the OPWC for Clean Ohio Green Space Conservation Program funds to receive

financial assistance for the completion of the Sugar Run Improvement Project and the

Darby Land Acquisition Project.

Section 2. The Village Administrator is hereby authorized to enter into any agreements as

may be necessary and appropriate for obtaining financial assistance through the Clean Ohio

Green Space Conservation Program.

Section 3. It is found that all formal actions of this Council concerning and relating to the

passage of this Resolution were adopted in an open meeting of this Council and that all

deliberations of this Council that resulted in this formal action were in meetings open to the

public in compliance with all legal requirements, including Section 121.22 of the Ohio

Revised Code.

Section 4. This Resolution shall be effective from and after the earliest period provided by

law.

MEMORANDUM

To: Members of the Village of Plain City Council

From: Haley Lupton, Village Administrator

Date: June 23, 2026

RE: Resolution 26-2026: Adopting the 2026 Plain City Comprehensive Plan Update

Background:

Pursuant to the direction of the Village Council, staff has engaged with the qualified consultant,

MKSK Studios, to complete thorough updates to the 2018 Plain City Comprehensive Plan. Through

exceptional research, public engagement, and stakeholder interviews, the finalized 2026 Plain

City Comprehensive Plan Update has been completed and is available for Council review and

adoption, if Council so desires. The finalized version of the plan is attached to Resolution 26-2026

in the form of Exhibit A. The plan advances and completes the Village Council goal of “continued

focus on smart planning and due diligence in regards to Village growth”.

Objective:

To adopt the 2026 Plain City Comprehensive Plan Update.

Recommendation:

Staff supports and recommends the passage of Resolution 26-2026 at the second reading on

August 10, 2026. The 2026 Plain City Comprehensive Plan has been completed through extensive

research and stakeholder input. Staff believes the plan will continue to support the Village

Council and staff in future decision-making and provides vital methodologies and

recommendations regarding a variety of influential topics affecting the Village.

800 Village Boulevard | P.O. Box 167 | Plain City, OH 43064 | 614-873-3527 | plaincityohio.gov

RESOLUTION NO. 26-2026

A RESOLUTION ADOPTING THE 2026 PLAIN CITY COMPREHENSIVE PLAN

UPDATE

WHEREAS, the Village of Plain City Council desires to maintain effective strategies and

plans to be used in support of future decision-making as it relates to the Village of Plain City

and welfare of its residents; and

WHEREAS, the Plain City Comprehensive Plan, previously adopted on December 17, 2018

through the passage of Ordinance 24-18, set forth vital tools and recommendations

designed to guide future land use, infrastructure improvements, recreational programming

opportunities, and various other topics as it relates to the Village of Plain City; and

WHEREAS, the Village Council determined the need to complete thorough updates to the

Plain City Comprehensive Plan to address the current and future growth and development

of the Village of Plain City; and

WHEREAS, pursuant to the Village Council’s determination, the 2026 Plain City

Comprehensive Plan Update has been completed, with such plan presenting a framework

and recommendations to guide policymaking and proactively manage growth and

development; and

WHEREAS, the Village Council recognizes the need and benefit of adopting the 2026 Plain

City Comprehensive Plan Update to guide decisions for future growth and development

within the Village and, upon review, has determined the need to adopt the 2026 Plain City

Comprehensive Plan Update.

NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED by the Council of the Village of Plain City, Ohio, a majority

of the members elected or appointed thereto concurring, as follows:

Section 1. The Village Council hereby approves and adopts the 2026 Plain City

Comprehensive Plan Update, as identified in Exhibit A, and attached hereto and

incorporated herein.

Section 2. It is found that all formal actions of this Council concerning and relating to the

passage of this Resolution were adopted in an open meeting of this Council and that all

deliberations of this Council that resulted in this formal action were in meetings open to the

public in compliance with all legal requirements, including Section 121.22 of the Ohio

Revised Code.

Section 3. This Resolution shall be effective from and after the earliest period provided by

law.
