Engineering Design Standards Page 6

Plan Review Process

2) Preliminary Development Plan

Items submitted during the Preliminary Development Plan process will receive a high-level

review of engineering-related items including site layout, pedestrian and vehicular circulation

and access, drainage, and other items of concern to the Village. The details submitted shall thus

show:

a) Proposed site layout.

b) Existing and proposed sidewalks.

c) Access points to public streets.

d) Right-of-way and public street widths.

e) Proposed stormwater runoff discharge points and detention basin locations.

3) Final Development Plan

Engineering items required within the Final Development Plan application shall provide more

advanced details of the overall site, utility, traffic, drainage, and property right improvements

necessary for the development to take place. As such, the following items are required:

a) A site plan showing:

i) Right-of-way line, centerline, lot lines, lot numbers, easements, street names,

development limits, and limits of construction.

ii) All proposed improvements and any modifications to existing public infrastructure.

b) A preliminary utility plan showing existing and proposed utilities and their sizes.

Note: Coordination with the Mid-Ohio Water and Sewer District on the establishment of

water and sewer service to the development is highly recommended ahead of this

submission.

c) A preliminary grading plan showing:

i) Proposed and existing contours

ii) Ditches and/or channels

iii) Overall drainage patterns

iv) Stormwater control measures

v) Discharge points

Engineering Design Standards Page 7

Village of Plain City, Ohio

d) A Preliminary Storm Water Study that includes:

i) An overall narrative of how stormwater runoff is managed throughout the site. This

includes an acknowledgement of the additional requirements established by

Appendix A of the Ohio EPA’s General Permit OHC000006 for projects within the

watershed of the Big Darby Creek, and a description of how these requirements will

be met.

ii) Calculations and exhibits showing whether stormwater detention is necessary per

the requirements of Section 4.8. This includes:

(1) Existing and proposed tributary area maps that show:

(a) Offsite and onsite tributary areas:

(b) Subcatchment tributary areas, showing:

(i) Boundaries, with consideration for project phasing, if applicable.

(ii) Acreages.

(iii) Assumed uses and corresponding runoff curve numbers and/or

coefficients.

(c) Existing and proposed storm and drainage structures.

(d) Proposed and existing contours.

(2) Critical storm calculations.

e) A traffic study, if deemed necessary by the requirements of Section 2.2.

4) Construction Plans

a) All Developments

Upon completion of the zoning review process, construction plans shall be submitted for the

development, or the phase of which is intended to be constructed. The list below details

submittal items required for all developments and is intended to communicate the level of detail

required within a plan submitted for review. Further requirements for construction plans are

included within parts b) and c) of this subsection, depending on whether the development has

substantial levels of public improvements. The Village may request additional details to help

facilitate their review if those submitted are not adequate to determine compliance with the

Village’s regulations.

i) Cover Sheet, including:

(1) The development name.

(2) An index map labeling major roads in the Village and indicating the project’s

location within the Village.

Engineering Design Standards Page 8

Village of Plain City, Ohio

(3) An overall plan view showing:

(a) The proposed improvements.

(b) Internal and adjacent property lines, rights-of-way, and their respective

owners.

(c) Adjacent road names.

(d) Major features within 100 feet of the development, including structures,

detention basins, streams, and ditches.

(4) Space for signatures of the Village Administrator, Mayor, Village Planner, and

Village Engineer.

ii) Village General Notes, detailed in Appendix A

(1) Notes that are not applicable to the development shall be replaced with “N/A.”

iii) An estimate of quantities for any public improvements, detailed by item numbers

from the City of Columbus Construction and Materials Specifications, where

applicable.

iv) Demolition details, if applicable.

v) Existing Conditions Plan

vi) Site Plan, showing:

(1) Right-of-way line, centerline, lot lines, lot numbers, easements, street names,

development limits, and limits of construction.

(2) All proposed improvements and any modifications to existing public

infrastructure.

vii) Grading Plan according to Section 4.2.

viii)Utility Plan, showing:

(1) All existing and proposed utilities, including:

(a) Water mains, their sizes, valves, and fire hydrants.

(b) All storm sewers and appurtenances.

(i) Identify storm sewer appurtenances by type.

(ii) All appurtenances shall be numbered.

(iii) Storm sewer appurtenances and locations should be referenced to the

station and offset of the centerline of the street.

(c) All sanitary sewers, their sizes, and appurtenances.

(d) Street lighting, including the type and location of all light poles, conduit, and

pull boxes.

Engineering Design Standards Page 9

Village of Plain City, Ohio

ix) Landscaping Plan, if applicable.

x) A Storm Water Management Report that includes plans, calculations, and reports

showing that the requirements of Section 4 have been met for projects constructing

drainage improvements. See Section 4.10 for further details.

b) Private Site Improvement Plans

Private Site Improvement Plans are required for developments which will construct

improvements that will primarily remain under private ownership. These plans shall include the

items in Part 4) a) of this section. The following may also be required depending on the nature

of the improvements:

i) Grading details showing compliance with accessibility requirements

ii) Stormwater Pollution Prevention Plan

iii) Stormwater detention details

iv) Utility profiles

v) Photometric plans

vi) Vehicle turning simulations for fire apparatuses. Please coordinate with the Tri

Township Fire Department on appropriate vehicle specifications to use.

c) Public Improvement Plans

Public Improvement Plans are required for developments which will construct improvements

that will remain under Village ownership after completion. These plans shall include the items in

Part 4) a) of this section as well as the following:

i) Plans constructing public roadways shall include:

(1) Typical sections

(2) Plan views showing:

(a) All contents required of the Site Plan in Part 4 a) vi) of this section.

(b) Centerline curve data, including delta, radius, arc, chord, and tangent.

(c) When proposed streets intersect with or join existing streets or traveled way,

indicate both edges of existing pavement, surface, or curb and gutter for a

minimum of 100 feet, or the length of connection, whichever is the greater

distance.

(d) Elevations along all curbs and curb returns on intersecting streets.

(e) Stations at every 100 feet on the centerline of proposed streets.

Engineering Design Standards Page 10

Village of Plain City, Ohio

(f) Stations of points of curve and tangent at the beginning and end of all returns

at centerline intersection, and at subdivision or section limits.

(g) The location of all or any natural springs, whether they are within or draining

to the right-of-way, and indicate proposed treatment of same. All springs will

be capped and piped in a minimum 6-inch diameter perforated pipe encased

in washed gravel, and connected into the nearest storm manhole or curb

inlet.

(h) The locations of traffic control devices, including signs and striping per the

requirements of Section 2 Part 1 t).

(3) Profile views showing:

(a) Elevations at beginning and end of all vertical curves.

(b) Length of vertical curves with elevations and stations of vertical points of

(c) Elevations computed every 50 feet on all tangent sections, and grades

computed every 25 feet in all vertical curves.

(d) Elevations at all storm sewer structures.

all streets that provide access to an adjoining property.

(f) Existing centerline profiles for 200 feet minimum distance to ensure proper

grade tie, when proposed street is an extension of, or connects with, an

existing street or road.

and left of proposed connection when a proposed street intersects with an

(i) All existing utilities, including crossings.

(j) Notes that may be necessary to explain the intent and purposes of the profile.

(4) Cross sections shall be submitted upon request by the Village Engineer. These

may be required when corridor sections are not standard, for example, when

topographic anomalies are encountered.

ii) Plans constructing public street lighting shall include plans and calculations showing

that the design meets the requirements of Section 3 of this manual.

iii) Plans constructing drainage improvements shall include plans, calculations, and

reports showing that the requirements of Section 4 have been met.

Village of Plain City, Ohio

5) As-Built Plans

Upon completion of construction of improvements under a Public Improvement Plan, the Owner

shall correct their plans to show to the “As-Built” conditions of their development and verify the

performance of their stormwater facilities.

i) Public improvements will not be accepted by the Village until these as-built

construction drawings and verifications are delivered and accepted.

ii) One (1) full size (22" x 34") copy, and one (1) half size (11" x 17") sets of drawings,

an electronic file in AutoCAD DWG format, and Excel spreadsheet in .xls format,

using a template accepted by the Village.

iii) The “As-Built” drawing shall show changes in the construction as recorded by a

Professional Surveyor registered in the State of Ohio.

iv) For projects constructing stormwater retention or detention facilities, a certification by

the Professional Engineer who prepared the plans shall be provided that verifies that

the retention/detention facilities were constructed and perform per the project’s

Construction Plans. Supporting calculations and reporting shall be submitted with

this certification.

Engineering Design Standards Page 12

Village of Plain City, Ohio

Section 2 – Public Streets

1) Public Street Design

a) All public streets shall be designed and constructed to meet the criteria established within

this section.

b) Private streets shall be designed to preserve the health, safety, and welfare of the public.

Designs for private streets are subject to the approval of the Village Engineer during their

review of the associated Construction Plan.

c) The determination of classifications for roads are guided by the Ohio Department of

Transportation Highway Functional Classification System or as determined by the Village.

d) Existing streets within the Village are classified as shown in Appendix B.

e) Roadway design standards are dependent on the projected traffic volumes for the road

being designed. This projection shall include all traffic generated by the development being

considered as well as the additional traffic generated by its interconnection to future

developments to ensure the adequacy of design as development progresses.

f) The Ohio Department of Transportation Location & Design Manual, Volume 1 – Roadway

Design (ODOT L&D Manual) shall be the basis of design for roads within the Village.

g) For roads with a traffic volume of less than 2,000 ADT (projected or actual), the AASHTO

Guidelines for Geometric Design of Low-Volume Roads, most recent edition, shall

supersede the requirements of the ODOT L&D Manual.

h) Right-of-way widths are dependent on the street classification and are as follows:

i. Arterial: 100 feet

ii. Major Collector: 80 feet

iii. Industrial: 80 feet

iv. Minor Collector: 70 feet

v. Local: 60 feet

i) If a proposed development fronts an existing roadway that does not meet the requirements

of this manual or the sections established with the Standard Drawings, the Village shall

require that it be improved to meet these standards. These standards include, but are not

limited to, right-of-way widths, street layout, and pavement composition.

j) The design and improvement of proposed and existing arterial roads shall be made on a

project-specific basis depending on the project being considered, the distribution of traffic,

and the uses accessed by the road. Any development along an arterial road will be required,

at a minimum, to provide the right-of-way width required by this section for an arterial road.

k) Pavement shall meet or exceed criteria outlined within the typical pavement section shown

within the standard drawings.

Engineering Design Standards Page 13

Village of Plain City, Ohio

l) Bridges shall be designed in conformance with the latest edition of the Ohio Department of

Transportation Bridge Design Manual. Calculations and designs shall be submitted to the

Village to demonstrate that these standards have been met.

m) Guard posts or barricades are required at the end of streets which are to be extended in the

future.

n) A T-turnaround shall be provided for dead-end streets exceeding 200 feet in length.

o) The distance between the centerlines of roads intersecting the same roadway shall be a

minimum of 150 feet apart.

p) Sidewalks and/or shared use paths shall be constructed per the Village’s standard drawings

along both sides of the road.

q) Sidewalks shall extend to the curbs at all intersections and shall include wheelchair ramps

meeting ADA requirements and the Village’s standard drawings.

r) Shared use paths may be required along property frontages and be constructed as per the

appropriate standard drawing.

s) Driveway aprons shall be constructed per the appropriate standard drawing.

t) All traffic, street name signs, and pavement markings shall be installed by the developer

a) Signing and striping shall conform to the requirements of the Ohio Manual of Uniform

Traffic Control Devices and the Village Engineer.

b) Street name signs shall be designed in accordance with the appropriate Village standard

drawing, unless otherwise allowed by the Village Engineer.

2) Traffic Impact Study

1) To promote efficient access management, a Traffic Impact Study (TIS) for a proposed

development shall be submitted when the development will generate more than 200 trips

in any peak hour, based on information obtained from the most recent version of the

Institute of Transportation Engineers (ITE) Trip Generation Manual.

2) The trip generation calculations shall be submitted with the Site Plan application or

Preliminary Development Plan, whichever is applicable.

3) Prior to the submission of a Traffic Impact Study, it is recommended that a Memorandum

of Understanding be agreed to with the Village that establishes the parameters and

assumptions of the study.

4) If required, the Traffic Impact Study shall be submitted with the Final Development Plan

(for PUD developments) or the Construction Plans (for non-PUD developments). Internal

trip estimates presented in terms of Average Daily Traffic shall also be included at these

stages to determine roadway classifications per Part 1) e) of this Section.

Engineering Design Standards Page 14

Village of Plain City, Ohio

Section 3 – Public Street Lighting

1) Street lighting shall be designed and installed by the developer following the City of

Columbus Street Lighting Design Guide, most recent edition, as modified by this section,

and is subject to the review, approval, and acceptance of the Village.

2) Light poles and luminaires are subject to the Village’s approval and shall be in accordance

with their current design standards.

3) Costs for powering light fixtures shall be the responsibility of the developer until the street

lighting system is accepted by the Village. Billing accounts shall be organized such that

each lighting control system receives its own bill for its corresponding lighting fixtures.

4) Lighting plans and calculations are required for the installation of all public street lights and

shall be subject to the following requirements:

a) Plan Contents

i) Lighting plans shall be prepared and sealed by a Professional Engineer registered in

the State of Ohio.

ii) A single-line diagram shall be provided that shows:

(1) The circuit routing through the development.

(2) All modifications to existing circuit routes.

(3) Stations and offsets of the following, relative to the centerline of the right-of-way:

(a) Transformers and controllers.

(b) Light poles

(c) Pull boxes

iii) A photometric plan shall be provided that shows:

(1) Illumination levels throughout the project.

(2) A summary schedule detailing:

(a) The average illumination and uniformity ratio.

(b) The target value established by the City of Columbus Street Lighting Design

Guide for each use (Roadway, Sidewalk, and Intersection).

(c) Separate lines on the summary schedule shall be provided for each use to

ensure compliance with the requirements for each use, including a separate

line for the sidewalk or shared-use path on each side of the road.

iv) Calculations detailing voltage drops shall be calculated per the City of Columbus

Street Lighting Design Guide.

Engineering Design Standards Page 16

Village of Plain City, Ohio

Section 4 – Drainage

1) Governing Standards

a) For items not covered within this manual, the Village may utilize the City of Columbus

Stormwater Drainage Manual and/or the Ohio Department of Natural Resource’s

Rainwater and Land Development guide to specify drainage and storm water

management practices within the Village.

2) Grading Plans

Where required, grading plans shall be submitted and show:

a) Ground elevations with existing and proposed contours shown at intervals of not more

than 5 feet where the slope is greater than ten percent (10%) and not more than 1 feet

where the slope is less than ten percent (10%).

b) Sufficient proposed elevations, such as at all lot corners, local high and low points, etc.,

to detail the proposed grading and drainage pattern. Additional details may be requested

if the grading design is not fully apparent.

c) Finished floor and ground elevations of all existing and proposed structures.

d) Flood routing across the site, including cross sections at critical points.

e) Elevations of proposed and existing storm sewer structures.

3) Site Drainage

a) Swales necessary to carry surface water must have a minimum gradient of one percent.

b) The minimum building elevation adjacent to the 100-year routing path shall be set a

minimum of 1 foot above the 100-year flood elevation. No basement entrances,

windows, or basement level garages shall be permitted adjacent to and below the 100-

year routing path.

c) The maximum desired slope in yard or open areas is 4:1.

Engineering Design Standards Page 17

Village of Plain City, Ohio

4) Easements

a) Flood routes on private property are required to be under easement.

i) Easements shall be at least equal to the area required to carry the 100-year rainfall.

ii) The easement shall be shown on the plat as a "Watercourse and Utility Easement."

Restrictions as to the use of this easement shall be shown on the plat.

iii) No buildings, plantings, or other improvements shall be permitted within that

easement.

b) Open watercourses shall be under easement.

i) Such easements shall incorporate area required to carry the 100-year rainfall plus an

additional 15 feet on each side of this area.

ii) These easements are to be kept free of obstructions.

iii) Detailed provisions regarding the entities to be responsible for maintenance of the

facility shall be submitted in text form with the plat. As applicable, notes regarding

maintenance shall be made on the plat.

5) Storm Sewer Design Criteria

a) The design of systems draining less than 200 acres of area shall be prepared using the

Rational Method and shall be based on a minimum 2-year frequency.

i) Rainfall intensity shall be per the National Weather Service’s Precipitation Frequency

Data Server for the project site.

ii) Runoff coefficients shall be as established by the City of Columbus Stormwater

Drainage Manual.

iii) Time of concentration shall be equal to the duration of time required for runoff to flow

from the most remote part of the drainage area to the point under design. The

minimum time of concentration shall be 10 minutes.

b) For drainage areas over 200 acres, the method explained in "Urban Hydrology for Small

Watershed, Technical Release No. 55" (known as TR-55) shall be used.

c) All areas which contribute storm water to the proposed drainage system must be

considered to determine of the sizes of structures and channels, including those entering

the system from offsite.

d) Storm sewer pipe sizes are to be determined by using Manning's Formula with a

minimum coefficient of roughness of n = .013.

e) Storm sewers shall be designed to provide a minimum allowable velocity of 3 feet per

second (fps) when the pipe flows full. The maximum allowable velocity shall be 7 fps.

Engineering Design Standards Page 18

Village of Plain City, Ohio

f) A hydraulic grade line check based on a 5-year storm may be required at the discretion

of the Village Engineer. The hydraulic grade line for the 5-year storm event shall stay

beneath the rim elevation of all structures along the storm sewer.

g) The minimum cover for storm sewers shall be 30 inches.

h) Pipe for storm sewers shall not be less than 12 inches in diameter and be designed for a

minimum of an HS-20 live load condition.

i) The flow lines of pipes shall be set such that either the crown or the 0.8 depth points at

junctions are at the same elevation. However, the crown of the outlet pipe may be lower.

j) Storm sewers connected to bodies of water shall be designed to ensure that upstream

structures do not carry standing water at normal water elevations.

6) Storm Sewer Layout and Construction

a) Storm sewer structures and construction practices are to be per the Village’s standard

drawings.

b) Headwalls or endwalls are to be constructed at the inlet and/or outfall of all storm sewers

and shall be shown on the plan and profile.

c) The inverts of all curb inlets, manholes, yard inlets, and other appurtenances shall be

formed to reduce turbulence to a minimum.

d) Manholes shall be provided at all changes in alignment and grade of storm sewers and

at such other locations as necessary to maintain a maximum interval of 300 feet

between manholes.

e) The maximum distance for overland flow shall be 300 feet before entering a surface yard

inlet or 400 feet before entering a curb inlet, except that the maximum overland drainage

area tributary to any yard inlet or curb inlet shall not exceed 1.5 acres. Structures

collecting off-site runoff are exempt from these requirements, but remain subject to the

flood routing and storm sewer performance requirements of this section.

f) The maximum spacing for curb inlets shall not exceed 400 feet unless approved by the

Village Engineer.

g) The maximum spread of flow during a 2-year storm shall not exceed 8 feet for 30-foot

wide streets and 9 feet for streets wider than 30 feet. Spread calculations may be

required at the discretion of the Village Engineer.

7) Open Watercourses

a) Where a 72-inch pipe is inadequate for a 2-year storm, an open watercourse may be

permitted.

Engineering Design Standards Page 19

Village of Plain City, Ohio

b) Design requirements:

i) The design storm shall be a 10-year storm, with a 1-foot freeboard.

ii) The minimum velocity for open channels shall be 2 feet per second.

iii) The maximum velocity is dependent on the type of channel protection provided.

iv) The minimum desirable grade for open channel is 0.4 percent (.4%).

v) The Manning "n" values for the channel lining shall be as detailed within the City of

Columbus Stormwater Drainage Manual, latest edition, or as required by the Village

Engineer.

vi) Side slopes shall be 3:1 or milder, with a minimum 2-foot bottom width. Slopes

adjacent to roadways shall be 4:1 or milder.

b) If the proposed improvements are located in a floodplain area as identified by the

Federal Emergency Management Agency, the limits of this floodway and floodway fringe

must be shown on the improvement plans.

8) Roadway Culverts

a) Single-span culverts, including concrete box or slab top, should always be considered in

lieu of multiple-cell pipe culverts in order to meet the physical requirements introduced

by rigid headwater controls.

b) The plan for each culvert shall indicate the drainage area in acres and the design

discharge in cubic feet per second.

c) The culvert invert elevation should be set such that it will be deep enough to provide an

adequate outlet for future storm sewer improvements upstream.

d) The design storm shall be as per Section 1103 of the ODOT Location and Design

Manual, Volume 2 – Drainage Design:

i. For traveled ways with 3000 ADT or more: 25-year.

ii. For traveled ways under 3000 ADT: 10-year.

iii. For pedestrian or bicycle routes: 5-year.

e) Culverts shall be designed in accordance with the Ohio Department of Transportation

Location & Design Manual, Volume 2 – Drainage Design.

f) Design calculations for all culverts shall be approved by the Village Engineer.

Engineering Design Standards Page 20

Village of Plain City, Ohio

Engineering Design Standards Page 21

(c) Assumed uses and corresponding runoff curve numbers and/or coefficients.

shows that the requirements of this section are met. This may include:

Section 5 – Construction Procedures

1) Pre-Construction Procedures

The following items are required to be submitted to the Village prior to beginning construction of

public improvements. If the Village requires a pre-construction meeting, the following must be

received at or before the date of the meeting. If a preconstruction meeting is not required, the

following must be received a minimum of 14 days prior to beginning construction. The submittal

will be processed upon receipt of all the following items, where applicable:

a. Original approved and recorded easements.

b. Copies of all regulatory permits (i.e. PTI, NOI, USACE Permit, Mitigation

Agreements, and Other permits specific to the project).

c. Storm Water Pollution Prevention Plan approved by the Ohio Environmental

Protection Agency.

d. Product material, manufacturer’s submittals, and shop drawings (pipe, manhole,

asphalt job mix formulas etc. manufacturers). These documents must be

reviewed and accepted by the design engineer prior to submission.

e. Signed Subdivider Agreement.

f. Construction bond or other financial construction surety (Developer’s Agreement

will contain specific requirements and amounts).

g. All fees paid, including inspection and plan review fees. (Developer’s Agreement

will contain specific amounts).

h. One digital and one hard copy of construction plans signed by all appropriate

authorities.

2) Acceptance of Public Improvements

a) Upon their completion of construction and testing of Improvements, the developer shall

request acceptance of the Improvements by the Village.

b) Upon this request, the Village may conduct an inspection and submit a list of corrective

items to developer for remedy.

c) Upon the developer’s completion and the Village’s acceptance of these remedies, the

developer shall provide:

i) "As built" drawings of the Improvements according to Section 1 Part 5.

ii) An Affidavit or waiver of lien from all Contractors or materialmen associated with the

project that all material and labor costs have been paid. The Owner shall indemnify

and hold harmless the Village from expenses or claims for labor or materials incident

to the construction of the Improvements.

Engineering Design Standards Page 23

Village of Plain City, Ohio

Engineering Design Standards Page 24

d) Upon completion of the steps above, the Village may provide Final Acceptance of the

v) As applicable, all necessary training to fully operate constructed Improvements

iv) Equipment, spare parts, operation & maintenance manuals, and additional

iii) A two-year maintenance bond.

warranties.

improvements.

Engineering Design Standards Page 25

Appendix A – General Notes

VILLAGE OF PLAIN CITY GENERAL NOTES:

1. The Village of Plain City requirements, together with the latest edition of the City of

Columbus, Construction and Material Specifications (CMS) shall govern all construction

items that are a part of this plan, unless otherwise noted. If there are any discrepancies,

the Village of Plain City requirements shall govern. All items referred to in these General

Notes are in reference to the City of Columbus Construction and Material Specifications,

unless otherwise noted.

2. All field testing required by the Columbus CMS shall be the responsibility of the

contractor, including, but not limited to material composition, material compaction,

concrete testing, etc.

3. Any modification to the specifications or changes to the work as shown on these

drawings must have prior written approval of the Village.

4. The contractor and subcontractor shall be solely responsible for complying with all

federal, state, and local safety requirements, together with exercising precautions at all

times for the protection of people (including employees) and property. It is also the sole

responsibility of the contractor and subcontractor to initiate, maintain and supervise all

safety requirements, precautions, and programs in connection with the work.

5. The contractor shall notify the Plain City Village Administrator's office (614-873-3527) at

least three working days prior to the commencement of any construction.

6. Construction may not begin until all required submittal items have been received and

accepted by the Village and a pre-construction conference, if required by the Village, is

held.

7. The Contractor shall obtain all necessary permits prior to construction.

8. The contractor is responsible for the investigation, location, support, protection, and

restoration of all existing utilities and appurtenances whether shown on these plans or

not. The contractor shall expose all utilities or structures prior to construction to verify the

vertical and horizontal effect on the proposed construction and shall make adjustments

in elevations to provide sufficient clearance between the proposed and existing utilities.

The contractor shall call the Ohio Utilities Protection Service (1-800-362-2764) and

Producers Underground Protection (1-800-925-0988) at least three working days prior to

work in the vicinity of their underground lines.

9. Any property corner pins or permanent survey markings disturbed during construction

shall be reset by a registered surveyor at the contractor's expense.

10. The tracking or spillage of mud, dirt, or debris upon Village streets is prohibited and any

such occurrence shall be cleaned up immediately by the contractor.

11. If the contractor fails to keep the work area clean of debris or fails to clean mud or dirt off

of Village streets, the Village may take action and assess the developer for the costs that

are incurred.

Engineering Design Standards Page 26

Village of Plain City, Ohio

12. No non-rubber tire vehicle shall be moved on Village streets. Exceptions may be granted

by the Village Administrator where short distances and special circumstances are

involved. Granting of exceptions must be in writing and any resulting damage must be

repaired to the satisfaction of the Village.

13. The contractor shall be responsible for maintaining mail service in the construction area.

14. All items of work called for on the plans for which no specific method of payment is

provided shall be performed by the contractor and the cost of same shall be included in

the price bid for the various related items.

15. The contractor shall exercise extreme caution when excavating in the vicinity of existing

trees, taking all measure possible to protect and preserve them. The contractor shall be

governed by the provisions of his contract with the owner.

16. All field tile broken during excavation shall be replaced to its original condition or

connected to the storm sewer system as directed by the Village.

17. Approval of these plans is contingent on all easements required for the construction of

the work being approved by the Village and recorded with the County prior to

commencement of the work. No work which requires an easement will be allowed to

proceed until this has been done.

18. All traffic control devices shall be furnished, erected, maintained, and removed by the

contractor in accordance with the Ohio Manual of Uniform Traffic Control Devices.

19. Steady burning, Type "C" lights shall be required on all barricades, drums, and similar

traffic devices in use at night. Cones are not permitted to be used for night work.

20. All traffic lanes shall be fully open to traffic on (street/drive) at all times except from 9:00

a.m. to 4:00 p.m. where one lane may be closed to traffic during those working hours.

21. All trenches shall be backfilled or securely plated during non-working hours.

22. Access to all properties along the project area shall be maintained at all times.

23. The developer/owner shall, prior to any construction operation, deposit with the Village

of Plain City a retainer for inspection, supervision, and testing of materials in the amount

of ten (10) percent of the estimated construction cost as approved by the Village.

24. If the Village Police Chief determines proper provisions for traffic control are not being

provided by the contractor, the Village Police Chief shall assign off-duty police officers to

the project at the contractor's expense.

25. All site clearing (tree removal) shall be performed by the owner at his expense prior to

construction of this project.

26. All areas within the public right-of-way that are disturbed by this project shall be re

stored to original or better condition, per Item 659 (Seeding and Mulching) or other

applicable specifications.

Engineering Design Standards Page 27

Village of Plain City, Ohio

27. At all utility crossings where the existing utility is exposed in the trench, the backfill shall

consist of compacted granular material in accordance with Item 912 between the deeper

and shallower pipe. Where proposed utilities or services cross proposed or existing

pavement areas, backfill shall be compacted granular material in accordance with Item

912 extending at least three (3) feet beyond the back of curb or edge of pavement. Cost

is to be included in the price bid for related pipe.

28. In the event excavation for the street is from 0" to 6" below that called for on the plans,

the Contractor shall replace this excess excavated material with Item 304 aggregate,

and shall be paid for by the Contractor.

29. The contractor shall provide two roof drain openings in the curb for each lot, each

opening located not more than four (4) feet in from each lot line.

30. It shall be the responsibility of the contractor to adequately barricade the street in the

vicinity of all expansion joints until such time as the street is open to traffic.

31. Where necessary to disturb pavements or drives, the pavement shall be saw cut in neat,

straight lines. The depth of saw cut shall be at least two (2) inches.

32. If the Contractor refuses or fails to perform work of an immediate nature (such as the

placement of barricades or replacement of signs or other devices) required by this

contract in a reasonable time, the Village will perform such work. The contractor shall re

imburse the Village at the rate of 2.5 times the actual cost of labor, materials, and

equipment necessary to perform such work. The Village shall notify or attempt to notify

the Contractor of the necessity to perform such work. The Village shall be reimbursed by

the Contractor in the amount provided herein. Reasonable time for all streets involved on

this contract is one (1) hour from the time of notification by the Village.

33. Prior to the construction of the streets, soil tests shall be made on all sanitary sewer and

designated storm sewer trenches which cross the proposed pavements, or which lie

such that the proposed pavements are located within any part of the influence line of

said trench. Where said results indicated that the trench backfill does not meet the

compaction requirements of Item 912, paragraph 912.03, all backfill material shall be

removed, replaced, and re-tested until compaction meets requirement of Item 912,

paragraph (j) 912.03.

34. Erosion control measures in accordance with the requirements of the Ohio EPA are

required. The Contractor shall provide sediment control at all points where water leaves

the project, including waterways, overland sheet flow and storm sewers. Accepted

methods of providing erosion/sediment control include but are not limited to inlet

protection, stabilized construction entrances, sediment basins, silt filter fence and

temporary ground cover.

35. The Contractor shall provide adequate drainage of the work area at all times consistent

with erosion control practices.

Engineering Design Standards Page 28

Village of Plain City, Ohio

36. Disturbed areas that will remain unworked for 21 days or more shall be seeded within 7

days of terminated work. Other sediment controls that are installed shall be maintained

until vegetation growth has been established. The Contractor shall be responsible for the

removal of all temporary sediment devices at the conclusion of construction but not

before growth of permanent ground cover.

37. A permit for construction activities that will disturb One (1) or more acres of land located

fully or partially within the Big Darby Creek Watershed must be obtained from the Ohio

EPA to allow for the discharge of stormwater from the construction site. Permitting

requirements shall be in accordance with National Pollutant Discharge Elimination

System (NPDES) Permit No. OHCD000006. It is the Contractor's responsibility to

comply with all requirements of this permit if the Contractor's disturbed area will equal or

exceed one (1) acre. In the terms of the permit, the Contractor shall be considered the

developer of the stormwater. The cost for all work involved in complying with these

requirements shall be included in the price bid for the various items of the contract.

38. The Village will not be responsible for means, methods, procedures, techniques, or

sequences of construction that are not specified herein. The Village will not be

responsible for safety on the job site, or for failure by the Contractor to perform work

according to contract documents.

39. Following completion of construction, the contractor shall submit documentation of as

built elevations, dimensions, slopes, and alignments of all elements of this project. The

as-built survey shall be prepared, signed and submitted by an Ohio professional

surveyor.

40. The Contractor shall restrict construction activity to public right-of-way and areas defined

as permanent and/or temporary construction easements.

41. The Contractor shall restore all disturbed areas to equal or better condition that existed

before construction. Drainage ditches or watercourses that are disturbed by construction

shall be restored to the grades and cross sections that existed before construction.

42. The Contractor shall not dispose of excess excavation at locations not approved by the

Village. Disposal outside project limits will not be allowed without submitting for review

and approval to the Village Engineer a signed agreement between the contractor and

the off-site landowner. An agreement should clearly state the purpose of the agreement,

show the location of the disposal area including floodplain and floodway boundaries,

display an estimate of volume of material to be disposed on the site, and indicate the

permission of the landowner for such use. Disposal within floodplains may not be

permitted within twenty (20) feet of floodway boundaries.

43. All signs, landscaping, structures, or other appurtenances disturbed or damaged during

construction shall be replaced or repaired to the satisfaction of the Village Engineer. The

cost of such work shall be paid for by the Contractor.

44. Permits to construct in the right-of-way of existing streets must be obtained from the

Village Administrator before commencing construction. Trenching, backfill, and

pavement requirements are found within the Village’s standard drawings and must be

adhered to.

Engineering Design Standards Page 29

Village of Plain City, Ohio

45. The contractor shall submit to the Village a City of Columbus-approved Job Mix Formula

for all bituminous mixtures to be placed on the project for review and approval. A design

mix formula shall be submitted for each mixture and each producer and shall provide

gradation of all component aggregates, percentage of blending of aggregates,

percentage of bitumen, any additives and application rate, names and addresses of

aggregate suppliers, Marshall mix design date and the theoretical laboratory density.

46. If allowed, the Contractor must obtain a blasting permit from Pleasant Valley Joint Fire

District before blasting for rock excavation may begin. The Contractor shall submit

blasting reports upon completion of blasting to the Village, the owner, and the owner's

engineer. Top of rock elevations shall be shown on as-built drawings.

47. Proposed utilities to be installed in embankment areas shall be placed after the

embankment has been placed and compacted according to the specifications.

48. When unknown or incorrectly located underground utilities are encountered during

construction, the contractor shall immediately notify the owner and the Village.

49. Before relocating any mailboxes, the Contractor shall contact the U.S. Postal Service

and relocate mailboxes according to the requirements of the postal service.

50. All benchmarks are based on United States Geological Survey (USGS) datum.

51. The location and identification of all public and private utility services shall be etched or

stamped in the face of the concrete curb

MEMORANDUM

To: Members of the Village of Plain City Council

From: Haley Lupton, Village Administrator

Date: July 22, 2026

RE: Resolution 37-2026: Amending the Planning and Zoning Fee Schedule

Background:

Village staff, pursuant to the provisions of Resolution 36-2026, which formalizes the

implementation of the Engineering Design Standards, recommends the amendment of the

Planning and Zoning Fee Schedule. Included within the Engineering Design Standards is the

Engineering Plan Review Fees which establishes fees related to the engineering review process

for a development. Currently, the Planning and Zoning Fee Schedule includes such fees. However,

with the proposed approval and implementation of the Engineering Design Standards, certain

engineering fees are no longer required to be included within the Planning and Zoning Fee

Schedule. Staff recommends the amendment and removal of certain fees from the Planning and

Zoning Fee Schedule to increase uniformity and standardization. The Planning and Zoning Fee

Schedule was approved and implemented through the passage of Resolution 34-2025 and

amended through Resolution 58-2025. As shown in Exhibit A as attached to Resolution 37-2026,

the proposed amendments include the following:

• Removing specific fees within the “Commercial/Industrial” section and referencing the

Engineering Plan Review Fees as detailed in the Engineering Design Standards.

• Removing and decreasing specific fees within the “Development” section and referencing

the Engineering Plan Review Fees as detailed in the Engineering Design Standards.

Objective:

To approve and implement an amendment to the Planning and Zoning Fee Schedule.

Recommendation:

Staff supports and recommends the passage of Resolution 37-2026 at the second reading on

August 24, 2026. The proposed amendment is in conjunction with the formal adoption of the

800 Village Boulevard | P.O. Box 167 | Plain City, OH 43064 | 614-873-3527 | plaincityohio.gov

unnecessary redundancy within processes administered by the Planning and Zoning Department.

Planning and Zoning Fee Schedule, in this manner, increases consistency and reduces

Engineering Design Standards and

800 Village Boulevard | P.O. Box 167

| Plain City, OH 43064 | 614-873-3527 | plaincityohio.gov

the Engineering Plan Review Fees. The amendment of the

RESOLUTION NO. 37-2026

A RESOLUTION AMENDING THE PLANNING AND ZONING FEE SCHEDULE

WHEREAS, the Village of Plain City, through the Planning and Zoning Department, is

responsible for administering the application and permitting process for various planning

and zoning services, including the imposition of fees related thereof as included in the

Planning and Zoning Fee Schedule; and

WHEREAS, the Village Council implemented the Planning and Zoning Fee Schedule through

the approval of Resolution 34-2025 and amended said Fee Schedule through the approval

of Resolution 58-2025; and

WHEREAS, Village staff, upon evaluation and pursuant to the adoption of the Engineering

Design Standards through the approval of Resolution 36-2026, recommends the

amendment of the Planning and Fee Schedule to amend fees associated with the

engineering plan review process to align with the provisions of the Engineering Design

Standards; and

WHEREAS,the Village Council concurs with the evaluation process and recommendation of

staff and has determined it necessary and appropriate to approve and implement an

amendment to the Planning and Zoning Fee Schedule as set forth in Exhibit A and attached

hereto and incorporated herein.

NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED by the Council of the Village of Plain City, Ohio, a majority

of the members elected or appointed thereto concurring, as follows:

Section 1. The Village Council hereby approves and authorizes the amendment of the

Planning and Zoning Fee Schedule, as identified in Exhibit A, and attached hereto and

incorporated herein.

Section 2. The Village Administrator is hereby authorized and directed to take any required

subsequent action to implement the amendment to the Planning and Zoning Fee Schedule.

Section 3. Where applicable, all Ordinances and Resolutions, or parts thereof, in conflict

with the provisions of this Resolution are hereby repealed and of no further force or effect.

Section 4. It is found that all formal actions of this Council concerning and relating to the

passage of this Resolution were adopted in an open meeting of this Council and that all

deliberations of this Council that resulted in this formal action were in meetings open to the

public in compliance with all legal requirements, including Section 121.22 of the Ohio

Revised Code.

Section 5. This Resolution shall be effective from and after the earliest period provided by

law.

Passed:
